Theorem 1

Statement: The Lasserre hierarchy of 153 has sample complexity via the martingale concentration bounds.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By computational complexity lower bounds via polynomial method, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

By computational complexity lower bounds via polynomial method, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎
